Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

I’ve been waking up with this heavy, hollow sadness. I’ve been uneasy sometimes, but I wouldn’t say worried all the time.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling hopeless?\n\nPatient: A little, maybe. It’s more like… I wake up and everything feels hollow. Like the lights are on, but there’s no music. It’s hard not to feel like it’ll stay that way.\n\nDoctor: Since this started, have you been feeling worthless?\n\nPatient: Yeah… somewhat. I’ve been waking up with this heavy, hollow sadness. Like… the studio lights are on, but there’s no music in me anymore.\n\nDoctor: Since this started, has your appetite changed?\n\nPatient: Yeah… somewhat. When I was really buzzing with ideas, I’d just forget to eat. And now when I do, it kind of tastes like cardboard. My rehearsal clothes feel looser lately.\n\nDoctor: Have you had thoughts that life isn’t worth living, or thoughts of harming yourself?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t really know how to answer that. I’ve felt pretty low, but… I don’t think I want to get into anything more than that right now.\n\nDoctor: Since this started, have other people noticed that you’re moving or speaking more slowly than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I’ve noticed it too. Even just crossing the studio to get my notebook feels… slow. Like I’m moving through wet sand or something.\n\nDoctor: Since this started, have you been feeling nervous or on edge a lot of the time?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. Maybe a little tense sometimes, but I wouldn’t say I’ve been nervous all the time.\n\nDoctor: Since this started, have you had a period where you felt unusually energetic or revved up?\n\nPatient: Yeah… there was a stretch where I felt kind of revved up. Even with the injury, I kept jumping from one thing to another—sketching maps, emailing people, reorganizing music files… like I had to redo the whole project all at once.\n\nDoctor: During that revved-up stretch, did you feel like you needed much less sleep than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah… during that stretch, I didn’t seem to need much. I’d stay up sketching formations until it was basically morning.
